   - The Mauser C96 was originally produced by Mauser from 1896 to 1937.
   - Mauser is a German arms manufacturer.
   - Unlicensed copies of the Mauser C96 were manufactured in Spain.
   - Unlicensed copies of the Mauser C96 were manufactured in China.
   - The unlicensed copies of the Mauser C96 were manufactured in the first half of the 20th century.
   [7.63×25mm Mauser]
   - The 7.63×25mm Mauser round was the original cartridge for the Mauser C96 service pistol.
   - The 7.63×25mm Mauser cartridge headspaces on the shoulder of the case.
   - The 7.63×25mm Mauser cartridge served as the basis for the 7.62mm Tokarev cartridge.
   - The 7.62mm Tokarev cartridge is commonly used in Soviet and Eastern Bloc weapons.

-- Facts
   [Jean de Florette]
   - Jean de Florette is a 1986 French period drama film.
   - Claude Berri directed Jean de Florette.
   - Jean de Florette is based on a novel by Marcel Pagnol.
   - Jean de Florette is followed by "Manon des Sources".
   - The film Jean de Florette takes place in rural Provence.
   - Two local farmers in the film plot to trick a newcomer out of his newly inherited property.
   - Gérard Depardieu starred in Jean de Florette.
   - Daniel Auteuil starred in Jean de Florette.
   - Daniel Auteuil won a BAFTA award for his performance in Jean de Florette.
   - Yves Montand starred in Jean de Florette.
   - Yves Montand's role in Jean de Florette was one of his last roles before his death.
   [Reassuringly Expensive]
   - Reassuringly Expensive was the Stella Artois' advertising slogan in the United Kingdom from 1982 until 2007.
   - The 1990s UK television advertising campaigns for Stella Artois imitated European cinema.
   - The leitmotif of the 1990s UK television advertising campaigns for Stella Artois was taken from the score of "Jean de Florette".
   - The score of "Jean de Florette" was inspired by Giuseppe Verdi's "La forza del destino".
   - The TV campaigns for Stella Artois began in 1991 with a series of adverts based on "Jean de Florette".
   - Anthea Benton and Vaughan Arnell directed the initial TV campaigns for Stella Artois.
   - The TV campaigns for Stella Artois included adverts based on war movies, silent comedy, and surrealism.
   - The slogan for Stella Artois was changed to "Reassuringly Elephants" for the surrealism-themed adverts.
   - Jonathan Glazer directed some of the TV campaigns for Stella Artois.
   - The aim of the TV campaigns for Stella Artois was to portray the drink in a context of sophisticated European culture.

-- Facts
   [The Mill (TV series)]
   - The Mill was a 2013 period television drama.
   - The Mill was broadcast on Britain's Channel 4.
   - The Mill is based on real-life stories and people of the textile mill workers at Quarry Bank Mill in Cheshire, England.
   - The Mill combines real-life stories with fictional characters and events.
   - The Mill was filmed in Cheshire.
   [Quarry Bank Mill]
   - Quarry Bank Mill is also known as Styal Mill.
   - Quarry Bank Mill is located in Styal, Cheshire, England.
   - Quarry Bank Mill is one of the best preserved textile mills of the Industrial Revolution.
   - Quarry Bank Mill is now a museum of the cotton industry.
   - Quarry Bank Mill is recorded in the National Heritage List for England as a designated Grade II* listed building.
   - Quarry Bank Mill inspired the 2013 television series "The Mill".

   [Louis Dunbar]
   - Louis Dunbar was born on August 8, 1953.
   - Louis Dunbar is also known as "Sweet Lou" Dunbar.
   - Louis Dunbar is the Director of Player Personnel.
   - Louis Dunbar is a coach.

   - Louis Dunbar played basketball for the Harlem Globetrotters for 27 years.
   [Harlem Globetrotters]
   - The Harlem Globetrotters are an exhibition basketball team that combines athleticism, theater, and comedy.
   - The Harlem Globetrotters have played more than 26,000 exhibition games.
   - The Harlem Globetrotters have played exhibition games in 122 countries and territories.
   - The Harlem Globetrotters' signature song is Brother Bones's whistled version of "Sweet Georgia Brown".
   - The Harlem Globetrotters' mascot is an anthropomorphized globe named Globie.
   - The Harlem Globetrotters play over 450 live events worldwide each year.
   - The Harlem Globetrotters are currently owned by Herschend Family Entertainment.
   - The executive offices for the Harlem Globetrotters are located in suburban Atlanta.

-- Facts
   [Julius Kahn (inventor)]
   - Julius Kahn was born in Münstereifel, Germany, on March 8, 1874.
   - Julius Kahn was raised in Detroit, Michigan.
   - Julius Kahn came to Detroit in 1881 from Germany with his family.
   - Julius Kahn was six years old when he came to Detroit.
   - Julius Kahn's family settled in Baltimore, Maryland in 1880.
   - Julius Kahn's father was Joseph Kahn.
   - Joseph Kahn was a rabbi.
   - Joseph Kahn ran a restaurant.
   - Joseph Kahn was born in 1860.
   - Joseph Kahn died in 1924.
   - Julius Kahn helped in his father's restaurant business.
   - Julius Kahn sold newspapers.
   - Julius Kahn's mother was Rosalie Kahn.
   - Rosalie Kahn was skilled in music and art.
   - Julius Kahn attended the Detroit Public Schools.
   - Julius Kahn received his elementary schooling at the Detroit Public Schools.
   - Julius Kahn completed his four-year high school in three years.
   [Trussed Concrete Steel Company]
   - The Trussed Concrete Steel Company was a company founded by Julius Kahn.
   - Julius Kahn was an engineer and inventor.
   - The Trussed Concrete Steel Company manufactured prefabricated products for reinforced concrete beams.
   - The Trussed Concrete Steel Company manufactured steel forms for building reinforced concrete floors and walls.
   - Julius Kahn invented and patented the Kahn System.
   - The Kahn System was a reinforcement system of construction.
   - The Kahn System was stronger than the existing technology used to construct buildings at that time.
   - The Kahn System was more economical than the existing technology used to construct buildings at that time.
   - The Kahn System was lighter than the existing technology used to construct buildings at that time.
   - The old method of constructing buildings used plain straight smooth steel beams or loose rods or stirrups in concrete beams and floors.
   - The Kahn System used 45 degree tab flanges or "wings" permanently attached on steel beams.
   - The 45 degree tab flanges or "wings" distributed the tension stress for overall improvement in strength of reinforced concrete.

-- Facts
   [Cedric the Entertainer]
   - Cedric Antonio Kyles was born on April 24, 1964.
   - Cedric Antonio Kyles is also known by his stage name, Cedric the Entertainer.
   - Cedric the Entertainer is an American actor, comedian, director, and game show host.
   - Cedric the Entertainer was the host on "It's Showtime at the Apollo".
   - Cedric the Entertainer hosted BET's "ComicView" during the 1993–1994 season.
   - Cedric the Entertainer hosted "Def Comedy Jam" in 1995.
   - Cedric the Entertainer co-starred with Steve Harvey on The WB sitcom "The Steve Harvey Show".
   - Cedric the Entertainer starred as Eddie Walker in "Barbershop".
   - Cedric the Entertainer hosted the twelfth season of the daytime version of "Who Wants to Be a Millionaire" in the 2013–2014 television season.
   - Cedric the Entertainer starred in the TV Land original series "The Soul Man".
   - The TV Land original series "The Soul Man" aired its fifth and final season in 2016.
   [Black Movie Awards]
   - The Black Movie Awards (BMAs) is an annual ceremony.
   - The Black Movie Awards recognize achievements of film actors of African descent.
   - The Black Movie Awards honor films that stand out in their portrayal of Black experience.
   - The Black Movie Awards was founded in 1997.
   - The inaugural Black Movie Awards event was held at the American Black Film Festival (ABFF).
   - The Black Movie Awards has been televised several times since 2005.
   - The 2005 Black Movie Awards ceremony was hosted by Cedric the Entertainer.
   - The 2006 Black Movie Awards ceremony was hosted by Tyler Perry.

   - Neil Corbould is an Academy Award winning special effect supervisor.

   - Paul Corbould is a brother of Neil Corbould.
   [Guardians of the Galaxy (film)]
   - Guardians of the Galaxy is a 2014 American superhero film.
   - Guardians of the Galaxy is based on the Marvel Comics superhero team of the same name.
   - Guardians of the Galaxy was produced by Marvel Studios.
   - Guardians of the Galaxy was distributed by Walt Disney Studios Motion Pictures.
   - Guardians of the Galaxy is the tenth film in the Marvel Cinematic Universe.
   - James Gunn directed Guardians of the Galaxy.
   - James Gunn wrote the screenplay of Guardians of the Galaxy with Nicole Perlman.
   - The film features an ensemble cast including Chris Pratt, Zoe Saldana, Dave Bautista, Vin Diesel, Bradley Cooper, Lee Pace, Michael Rooker, Karen Gillan, Djimon Hounsou, John C. Reilly, Glenn Close, and Benicio del Toro.

-- Facts
   [My Wicked Heart]
   - "My Wicked Heart" is a song co-written and co-produced by Diana Vickers.
   - "My Wicked Heart" was digitally released on 17 October 2010.
   - "My Wicked Heart" was physically released on 1 November 2010 by RCA Records in the United Kingdom.
   - The track "My Wicked Heart" was inspired by indie music including The xx and The Doors.
   - Diana Vickers plays the trumpet on the track "My Wicked Heart".
   - Diana Vickers performed "My Wicked Heart" live on the seventh series of "The X Factor" on 17 October 2010.
   - "My Wicked Heart" peaked at number 13 on the UK Singles Chart.

   [The xx]
   - The xx are an English indie pop band.

   - The xx were formed in Wandsworth, London.
   - The xx currently consists of Oliver Sim, Romy Madley Croft, and Jamie xx.
   - Oliver Sim plays bass and vocals for The xx.
   - Romy Madley Croft plays guitar and vocals for The xx.
   - Jamie xx plays beats, MPC, and record production for The xx.
   - The xx are known for their distinct and unique minimalistic sound.

   [The Santa Clause]
   - The Santa Clause is a 1994 American Christmas fantasy family comedy film.
   - The Santa Clause was directed by John Pasquin.
   - The Santa Clause is the first installment in "The Santa Clause trilogy".
   - The Santa Clause stars Tim Allen as Scott Calvin.
   - Scott Calvin is an ordinary man who accidentally causes Santa Claus to fall from his roof on Christmas Eve.
   - Scott Calvin has a young son named Charlie.
   - Scott Calvin and Charlie finish St. Nick's trip and deliveries.
   - Scott Calvin goes to the North Pole.
   - Scott Calvin learns that he must become the new Santa.
   - Scott Calvin must convince those he loves that he is indeed Father Christmas.
   [Rebecca Forstadt]
   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt was born on December 16, 1953.
   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt is also known as Reba West.
   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt is an American voice actress.
   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt studied theater at Orange Coast College.
   - Orange Coast College is located in Costa Mesa, California.
   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt worked at Knott's Berry Farm's Bird Cage Theater.
   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt performed melodramas at Knott's Berry Farm's Bird Cage Theater.
   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt often played the damsel in distress character at Knott's Berry Farm's Bird Cage Theater.

   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt worked as a wardrobe mistress on "Hill Street Blues".
   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt worked as a wardrobe mistress on the film "S.O.B.".
   - Rebecca Lynn Forstadt did live theater in the Los Angeles area.

   - KSCW-DT is a CW-affiliated television station.
   - KSCW-DT is licensed to Wichita, Kansas, United States.
   - KSCW-DT is owned by Gray Television.

-- Facts
   [Laurence Clarkson]
   - Laurence Clarkson was born in 1615.
   - Laurence Clarkson died in 1667.
   - Laurence Clarkson was sometimes called Claxton.
   - Laurence Clarkson was born in Preston, Lancashire.
   - Laurence Clarkson was an English theologian.
   - Laurence Clarkson was accused of being a heretic.
   - Laurence Clarkson was the most outspoken and notorious of the Ranters.
   - The Ranters were a loose collection of radical Protestants.
   [Protestantism]
   - Protestantism is a form of Christianity.
   - Protestantism originated with the Reformation.
   - The Reformation was a movement against errors in the Roman Catholic Church.
   - Protestantism is one of the three major divisions of Christendom.
   - The other two major divisions of Christendom are Roman Catholicism and Orthodoxy.
   - The term "Protestantism" derives from the letter of protestation from German Lutheran princes in 1529.
   - The letter of protestation was against an edict of the Diet of Speyer.
   - The edict of the Diet of Speyer condemned the teachings of Martin Luther as heretical.

-- Facts
   [MGM Grand fire]
   - The MGM Grand fire occurred on November 21, 1980.
   - The MGM Grand fire occurred at the MGM Grand Hotel and Casino (now Bally's Las Vegas) in Paradise, Nevada, USA.
   - The MGM Grand fire killed 85 people.
   - Most of the people killed in the MGM Grand fire died through smoke inhalation.
   - The MGM Grand fire is the worst disaster in Nevada history.
   - The MGM Grand fire is the third-worst hotel fire in modern U.S. history.
   - The 1946 Winecoff Hotel fire in Atlanta killed 119 people.
   - The 1946 Winecoff Hotel fire in Atlanta is the worst hotel fire in modern U.S. history.
   - The Dupont Plaza Hotel, San Juan, Puerto Rico fire occurred on December 31, 1986.
   - The Dupont Plaza Hotel, San Juan, Puerto Rico fire killed 97 people.
   - The Dupont Plaza Hotel, San Juan, Puerto Rico fire is the second-worst hotel fire in modern U.S. history.
   [Winecoff Hotel fire]
   - The Winecoff Hotel fire occurred on December 7, 1946.
   - The Winecoff Hotel fire was the deadliest hotel fire in United States history.
   - The Winecoff Hotel fire killed 119 hotel occupants.
   - The hotel's owners were among the victims of the Winecoff Hotel fire.
   - The Winecoff Hotel was located at 176 Peachtree Street in downtown Atlanta, Georgia, United States.
   - The Winecoff Hotel was advertised as "absolutely fireproof".
   - The Winecoff Hotel's steel structure was protected against the effects of fire.
   - The Winecoff Hotel's interior finishes were combustible.
   - The Winecoff Hotel's exit arrangements consisted of a single stairway serving all fifteen floors.
   - All of the hotel's occupants above the fire's origin on the third floor were trapped.
   - The fire's survivors were either rescued from upper-story windows or jumped into nets held by firemen.
   - The fire resulted in a number of victims who jumped to their deaths.
   - A photograph of one survivor's fall won the 1947 Pulitzer Prize for Photography.
   - The La Salle Hotel fire in Chicago occurred on June 5, 1946.
   - The La Salle Hotel fire in Chicago resulted in 61 fatalities.
   - The Canfield Hotel fire in Dubuque, Iowa occurred on June 19, 1946.
   - The Canfield Hotel fire in Dubuque, Iowa resulted in 19 fatalities.
   - The Winecoff Hotel fire spurred significant changes in North American building codes.
   - The changes in North American building codes included requiring multiple protected means of egress.
   - The changes in North American building codes included requiring self-closing fire-resistive doors for guest rooms in hotels.
